                                 llJBBOCK, TEXAS                           •

                                 25 August 1951                                                 I
                                                                                                I
                                                                                                            •
On the evenings of 25 August 1951, 31 August 1951 , and l Sept ember 1951 , f lights
of unidentified objects were observed over Lubbock, Texas; in some instances there
vere s everal flights per evening. The des cr ipt ion of the objects va r ied t o some
extent, but it is generally agreed that i t vas a "V" or "U" s haped set of l i ght-
l i ke objects. Mr           , an amateur photographer, turned over photographs to              I
the Ai r Force which he claims a r e of two of thefligbts . Disagreement among t he
witnesses is on the number of lights in the arranpent and whether it wa s "V" or
"U'' shaped. The flight path vas from NE1:D SW or N-S. The obje ct appea r e d brighter
than stars and bad a ye l low or b l ue tint. In most cases no nois e ~a s r eported,
but a few witnesses stated they heard a "whoosh."

Many witnesses reported the incident, but the most t echnically compet ent observers
      probably Profes                 Head of                           Dr
         Professor of                         and Dr                Profe ssor of
           all of Texas Tech.     addition t o being the most compet ent , the s e wi t -
nesses were among the first to see the object and viewed it on subsequent occasions             1
under controlled circumstances. For their initial sighting the profe ssor s were
gathered together discussing astronomy on the evening of 25 August 1951. At 2110
they happened to observe the first flight of the object, which they describ ed as
a rough semi-circle of lights. A s e cond object passed ove r three mi nutes later.
The witnesses estimated the angul ar ve locity to be 30° per second. During subse-
que nt observations the professors confir111ed their estimate of the velocity by usi ng
protra cto rs and stop wa tches. Attempts were also made t o get an esti mate of the
eleva t ion by viewing the object from seve r a l locations at the same t i me a nd using
trigonometric methods, and also by using clouds of known he ight. The se atte mpts
we re not considered successful .

In 1959 Dr J Allen Hynek contacted one of the professors at Texas Tech r egarding
this case. This professor informed Dr Hynek that he had conducted an e xtensive
study of the Lubbock sighting and determined that they were definitely birds.

The Air Force conclusion is that birds, with street lights reflecting from them,
vere the probable cause of this sighting. The angular vel oc ity of 30° per second                  I
s e ems r ather high for birds during migratory flights (approximately 6o MPH at 168
f eet altitude}. It is probable that the angular velocity vas less. In a ll insta nces
the v itne sses were located in an area where their eyes were probably dark adapted,
the refore making the objects appear brighter.

Mr         when taking his pictures, had to do so by "panning" his camera {swinging
wi th t he object's motion}. Panning is quite difficu l t and the r e l&ti vely h igh
de gree of success of this photographer is fu r the r indication t hat t he angular ve locity
of the ob j ect was not a s hi gh as estimated .

The k i nd of birds respons i b le f or this s ight i ng i s not known, but i t is h i ~~ ly
probab le that t hey we r e ducks or pl overs. Since plovers do not u sually f ly i n f or -
nations of mor e t han six or s even, ducks become t he more pr obable . The fact tha t
thi s w ~o late summer, and that t he obj e cts consist ently flev to the s outh t e nds t o
subs tant iate the concl us ion that the ob jects of t his sighting wer e migr a tory birds .

                      The bulk of the following information i s t aken from photostatic co~ies

            of a rticle from the Lubbock, Texas, Avalanche Journal, dates 26 Aur,us t 1951,                                                   •

            ~8    AuRUSt 1951, 1 September 1951, 2 September 1951, and 5 Seotember J951 .                                 The
    •

                      On the   evening of 25 August 1951, 31 August 1951, and 1 September 1951,
            flights of an unidentified object were observed over Lubbock, Texas, in some

            instances there were several fli ghts per evening.                    The descri?tion of the objects

            varies to some extent but it i s generally agreed that it is a V or U-~haped

            set of lights.         Photos were obtained of two such flights.                            (See inclosure #1.)

            The photographer,                               has been investigated and appears reliable.

            Disagreement on the description of the obj ect is in t he number of l i ghts, which
            varies from 50 to 18, and whether it was a V or U- sho.oe.                                 'l'he fli ght path was

            ?;t, to SW or N to s.         They were brighter than stD.rs and yellowish or blue.                           In

            most cases there vs.s an absence of noise, a few people heard a slight "whoosh''•

                      The incident has aroused the populace of Lubbock and two t heories have

            arisen.        One is that it is an aircraft; the other is that it i s light reflecting

            off some type of migratory fowl.

                      A large number of people have vi tnes s ed the incident.                           The ones who have

            been named in renorts are listed in inclosure #2, but many other unnamed people
            obser1ed the incident on various nights.                   Probably the oost technically com-
            ?etant observers were:                                                                                                ,
                      a.                , Professor and Head of ?etrole~~ Engineering
                            Ceoartment at Texas Tech.

                                  'Strange Aircraft'
                              1
                                  Seen At Matador
                                                     --·
                                      MATADOR, ( Aug. 31..';(Spe-
                                   cial> A "noiseless aircraft,
                                   flying at a low altitude with-
                                   out a i d of propellors or,
                                   wings," w a s reported seen
                                   early this afternoon by two
                                   r~atador district women and
                                   a 5-year-old child.
                                      Mrs. Tom T i 1 s o m, her
                                   daughter Mrs. M. G. Bethard                                                                              •


                                   and little Noilene Bethard
                                   said they were driving on
                                   State Highway 70, one and
                                   one-half miles north of here
                                   about 12:45 p. m. when the
                                   wingless craft passed 150 feet                                                               •
                                                                                                                                    •
                                   in front of the car.          -                                                      '
                                                                                                                            '
                                                                                                                    •
                                      The slow-moving machine,                                                      •




                          •
                                   "shaped somewhat like a heli-                                          • •
                                   copter," began circling as Mrs.
                                   Bethard stopped the car. As
                                   the craft rose it "gained speed/
                                   and was out of sight within
                                   a few minutes."
                                      The w o m e n were near
                                   enough to spot one door, or
                                   porthole, in the side of the                                                 •
                                   glean1ing metal, they said.
                                   Whe first seen, it was mov-
                                   ing t the rate of a com-
                      •
                                   mere· al airliner taking off,                                      •

                                   they · said. It had no exhaust
                                   sho ing.
                                      M . Bethard, whose hus-
                                   band sailed for Korea recent-
                                   ly, h s lived near several Air
                                   Fore bases, and reported the
                                   mac ine she saw today. was                                                                               •
                                   diffc ent from any she had                                                                           •

                                   seen before.
